ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e3213	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Since the 1970s, there have always been words that should be magic in education policy. The question now arises: 82/M “What measures of the educational reform adopted in the previous year, in particular the included autonomy package, have already been initiated or implemented, which steps in relation to these are still open?”
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e3234	Mr Fassmann, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e3249	Thank you very much for the question! The Education Reform Act is linked to the establishment of education directives. This is an essential step. If you want to do so, these education directorates are a one-stop shop, where both the federal and the state affairs are perceived. We are currently making these education directorates operational at 1.1.2019, we have appointed the educational directors and the department heads. We are also in the process of bringing to the attention of the educational directors, who have a difficult position between the national interests and the federal interests, a certain loyalty to both the one and the other. We regularly make Jours fixes with them. We are currently in the process of rescheduling school supervision. What may still be missing from the education reform is a single control system. On your second detailed question, the matter with school autonomy: The legislator is convinced that certain local decisions can be made better. I can certainly join this, the school and the teachers there know very well what to do. As part of the school autonomy, there is a autonomy of class design, but also class size. There is a flexibility in the duration and opening hours of the school. A school can, if it is necessary regionally, also open at 7 a.m. There is the possibility of autonomous priority education. There is also – which is very important – a certain autonomy of personnel. Schools can then choose teachers depending on how well a teacher person fits his own school profile. So we are on the right track, both in the area of the establishment of educational directorates and in the area of school autonomy, and I think it is a very useful way.

The small communities are of the opinion: We also carry out essential tasks, in difficult conditions, often also in the case of declining population, we Therefore, more resources are needed and, if you want to do so, a reversed population key. There is a certain dissertation that is not easy to resolve. The Ministry of Finance has therefore asked whether this § 15 of the Financial Balance Act is still necessary. The answer of the countries was: No, no, no, The answer was mediated by the head of state of the Burgenland, and this was taken out. They have also been taken out – to add it the same way – because resource allocation, also a resource allocation depending on the socio-economic status of parents, is re-regulated in the Education Reform Act. Therefore, § 15 of the Financial Balance Act is no longer necessary at all.

Pre-school education has so far been compulsory. Now there can be cases where someone makes one, two class repetitions. This means that, in addition to the pre-school year completed, it is well before the time when you are faced with the termination of your compulsory school, even if you have actually finished the school or not. School law is also called school law, and if you take it out, then there is really the right to be able to attend nine years of school, regardless of preschool. It is actually a positive measure.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e4923	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e4939	We have a system with two different access schemes. One is a national regulatory system in fields of study such as architecture, biology, education, foreign languages, computer science, management, pharmacy, publicity and law. This is throughout the country, because national figures are defined and it is negotiated with the universities who take on what share as well. There is also the possibility of location-specific regulations. So if a university says that only with it does a very strong demand for certain courses occur, it can make a location-specific access management in coordination with the Ministry. This concerns some universities in some fields, such as political science at the University of Vienna or sociology at the University of Vienna. Thus, both together help to create an improved study situation in line with increased demand in certain subjects.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e6134	Federal Minister, please.

So major projects are to be drawn up to master very concrete things. Under the motto: A Europe that protects, we have focused on three areas in particular. – First of all: Of course, protection is security and therefore also the fight against illegal migration. A Europe that protects must also ensure the standard of living that we have built in Europe, which means a focus on competitiveness, on the economic strength of the European Union. And a Europe that protects must export security and not import uncertainty, i.e. to become active beyond our European territories, beyond our borders at the Western Balkans and in other regions of the world. I would like to express my satisfaction at the first point in migration that we started a trend change at European level in June, away from the purely distribution debate, away from the blockade between opponents and distributors to a focus on other areas of migration. Not only the debate on how we deal with migrants within the European Union, but above all the debate on how to fight illegal migration has also been focused on our efforts. In June, the decisions we have taken in the European Council were a turning point: a focus not only on distribution, but above all on external border protection, on cooperation with third countries. I am pleased that, under the chairmanship of Minister for the Interior Kickl, it has succeeded in making a decision on Frontex possible to strengthen it, especially in the case of repatriation, but also in the case of cooperation with third countries. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Plessl.]] As far as the distribution debate is concerned, it is But from our point of view, to be very positive, that the Commission has now presented a proposal, a paper which is almost entirely in line with our proposals for mandatory solidarity. In this context, I am glad that we were able to open a completely new chapter here: with cooperation with Egypt, but also with other North African states, which are now more and more carrying out rescues themselves, but after the rescue in the Mediterranean, bring people back not to Europe but to the transit countries – with the result that illegal migration to Europe is falling significantly. Overall – and I think figures are crucial – there is a 95 percent drop in arrivals in the European Union compared to 2015; and most importantly, the number of deaths, the people drowning in the Mediterranean, has also fallen dramatically. This is about market access, fair rules for drivers and transport companies, but also effective controls for national authorities; and above all, it is about ensuring better working conditions as well as greater legal certainty for companies that, of course, operate in several Member States. ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e13436	We're now on the 5th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first is Mrs Holzleitner's speech. - Here you go.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e13464	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! Education is still a major cost factor in Austria and, above all, a financial burden at the start of school. This financial burden is often immense for families in particular. School materials, crafts, printing, school trips – the list of purchases is long. In order to be able to accurately picture these challenges, a current survey of school costs, which is also regularly updated – best in three to five years – would be very welcome. Not only in the field of school, but generally, a child cost analysis in Austria would be overdue again. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Cox.]] But why does such analysis need to be done? – The answer is actually clear: to raise and represent the actual costs for every student and every student and every school level and, subsequently, to support the families and, above all, the children as best as possible. If you know where the shoe is pressing, then you can of course adjust the promotions and support optimally. A school cost survey of the AK has shown that the costs in the primary school per school year and school child are about 855 euros. The school costs for a child in the upper level of the gymnasium are already EUR 1 299. It is about education, but it is also about social and social participation in school trips, museum visits, hiking days et cetera. Children may not be affected by stigmatisation due to the stock exchange rules of their parents. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[call to the FPÖ.]] A cost survey helps to show how strong parents are really burdened by their children’s schooling. Such a survey will also help to better apportion support services such as school start-up fees. Of course, it should be borne in mind how often this cost collection is carried out and what criteria should be taken into account so that one has a representative sample and can also implement it with a bureaucratic effort. We would in principle welcome this measure. I would just like to say in general that we as social democracy are particularly committed to free education, because education is extremely important – important for the future of children and young people in our country – and access to education is definitely not to be dependent on the exchange rate and on the financial situation of parents.

In addition, the cost of the pupils, as I believe, is already very time-consuming. This would result in a very, very high red tape for teachers. I would rather save our teachers. But in itself I give you and you are right, because it is already that the costs for the school are rising very much, that is true – the AK has carried out corresponding calculations. However, when we generally look at the situation in society, in families with children, we see that usually others also incur very high costs, which you can or must see in relation with them. When you think of mobile phones, clothing and so on, it turns out that the values have already shifted. That is why I would also like to move my speech in this direction. In the previous debate, the school start-up package has already been discussed: There is already this support on the part of the federal government through the start-up package. That is 100 euros that are automatically paid out with the child allowance in September. It is also to be welcomed and greatly appreciated that almost all federal states offer further support. The highest costs usually come when the students go to the project week, to the ski course or to the sports week. That's where the costs are high. Countries are very creative and also provide needs-oriented support because of the number of children. Some countries can even come up with a lot of things, especially in the tourist sector, for example, that children don't have to pay for lift tickets if they stay in the federal state, and so on and so on. So there are really a lot of support that are very remarkable. I think the request is generally very positive, but it is a little poorly thought out and – as is often the case with the opposition – unfortunately made under the lack of involvement of parents, educators and students. Thank you.

What's this about? – This is a reporting procedure for foreign courses offered in Austria. The old reporting procedure – I am now going to shorten it – has been abolished by the Constitutional Court as unconstitutional, and a new law had to be passed within the deadline until 31.12.2018, otherwise we would have had a gap. We have succeeded in a corresponding parliamentary process, which, as my previous speakers have already said, can certainly be regarded as a model example of functioning parliamentarism – to the extent that a presentation has been submitted by the government parties to the committee, suggestions and improvements have been put to the discussion by the opposition parties, and that the government parties, yes, in fact, all the suggestions from the committee in the form of an all-party amendment proposal, are now making legislation. Mrs Kuntzl has already said it: an increased publication obligation for the institutions offering it in so far as it points to comparability, which does not mean equivalence – so here we are talking about foreign academic degrees being awarded. The whole thing is also appropriate, I quote, to communicate “in written and visually highlighted form” by the providers. The ombudsman for students is now also inside, and the transitional period has been shortened from six to five years. As chairman of the Scientific Committee, I continue to thank all members of the Scientific Committee for this constructive process.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e18736	Next word comes Mrs. Dr. Jessi Lintl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-12-12-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00055_d7e18760	Madam President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The most important at the novelle of the dormitory law is that our government finally creates legal certainty after 20 years – both for students and for home operators. Since the last novel of the Student Home Act in 1999, the housing market for students has changed. There is a growing number of so-called commercial – i.e. non-profit – student home managers. These had not yet to comply with the provisions of the Student Home Act. Residents of such homes had only insufficient legal protection, simply those according to the General Civil Code. As a result of this legal loophole, it was so far possible to circumvent the provisions of the Student Home Law to the detriment of the residents of the home. This gap has now been closed on the initiative of the Federal Government. The aim of the new Student Home Act is to capture all kinds of student homes in the future. The new definition of home and student home operators is intended to ensure that the rental of homes to students is at least covered by the Student Home Act. Students have become more mobile in the last decades. You will often change the place of study or the accommodation. They are more flexible and therefore use contracts should be made more flexible. The new Student Home Act addresses precisely these points. Students are no longer bound to the contracts for so long. They may also withdraw or withdraw for a period of less than one year. In addition, a ceiling has been introduced for the collection of deposits. Deposits may not exceed twice the user fee. The new Student Home Act therefore provides an appropriate response to the developments of recent years and, I believe, a clear improvement for students. Thank you, Minister.

That's a horrifying sum for a studying person! This difference can be traced back to a law gap. In principle, the Student Home Law provides for cost coverage. Operators may break out of it due to their own statutes. However, this provision cannot be seen in the law. Instead of now closing and repairing this gap with the new law, so that all are subject to the cost-covering principle, the whole law is driven to the wall. Two pillars of the law fall. One has already been mentioned, namely that the students have so far been significantly involved in home status and home order. They are now making it difficult for them to co-determination. In the event of conflicts, it was so that they were could solve internally, now an external, expensive mediator is ordered. This falls the pillar of co-determination. So far, the cost recovery principle has been applied. Now a free rent is possible after the promotion for non-profit and private providers. Thus, students are degraded to quite normal customers, whose economic situation is no longer taken into account at all. In short, a new investment form will be created for investors: free rents with special periods of time, as the term will normally be at least three years. All of this seems to me to be a punishment for academics, where FPÖ only enjoys 7 percent voter approval. Why the ÖVP carries this and participates in it, I can only explain to myself with the interests of the real estate and large donors. This means that a young officer receives 3.38 percent increase and 63 euros more per month in the first grade. In particular, numerous proposals have been made on the part of the Freedom Economy in the area of electoral law. Among other things, a legal clarification could be reached as regards the list of voters' groups and, in particular, a reduction in the minimum number of explanations of support necessary for the submission of election proposals. Other improvements, such as the legal status of self-employed voters and voters, have also been implemented. The composition and responsibilities of the main electoral committees have been clarified. The data protection status of the electorate groups represented in the Economic Parliaments has been improved. Based on the findings of the Federal Presidential Election 2016, a system adopted by the Federal Government in the field of electoral card envelopes was introduced. As a result of the tenacity of the political groups, the entire Chamber of Commerce has already undergone a reform of the WKO 4.0, with the reduction of chamber charges 1 and 2, in concrete terms, a relief from the basic amount in the year of foundation, the elimination of the multiple basic conditions within a section and so on. In this respect, we are talking about a discharge of 63 million euros for entrepreneurs. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, this is a first step. It is a first step in the right direction, and I ask you to support the request. Thank you.

[[Call the ÖVP:Ready time!]] This judgment was then withdrawn by the Supreme Court in Luxembourg and referred back to the second instance.
